<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every coloring of the Euclidean plane with five colors has a monochromatic unit-distance pair, with no regularity assumption on the color classes. Consequently, the chromatic number of the plane is either six or seven.

- **claim-plane-not-five-colorable** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The Euclidean plane has no proper five-coloring, even when arbitrary color classes are allowed. Consequently, $$6\le \chi(\mathbb R^2)\le 7.$$
  - Explanation: An arbitrary assignment of five labels to the Euclidean plane cannot exclude every monochromatic unit-distance pair. The supplied hexagonal construction gives the seven-color upper bound; the theorem leaves six and seven as the remaining alternatives.
- **claim-proper-weak-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-transfer)): For every positive integer $k$, in ZFC, $$\label{eq:source-1} \begin{gathered} \text{a proper $k$-coloring of $\mathbb R^2$ exists} \\ \Longleftrightarrow \\ \text{a weak measurable $k$-coloring of $\mathbb R^2$ exists}. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: For each finite positive color count, existence of an unrestricted proper coloring is equivalent to existence of a weak measurable coloring. This is an existence equivalence in ZFC, rather than a pointwise conversion of a given weak coloring into a proper coloring.
- **claim-no-weak-five-coloring** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-weak-five)): There is no weak measurable five-coloring of the Euclidean plane.
  - Explanation: The product-measure relaxation still cannot be satisfied with five labels. The proof extracts an interface cycle and excludes every possible cycle length.
- **claim-positive-five-color-badness** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-positive-badness)): There is a constant $\delta>0$ with the following properties.* 1. *For every coloring $c:\mathbb R^2\to\{1,\ldots,5\}$ and every normalized finitely additive left-invariant measure $\mu$ on all subsets of the plane-isometry group $E(2)$, the invariant-mean badness satisfies $$p_5^\mu(c):=\mu\bigl(\{T\in E(2):c(T^{-1}0)=c(T^{-1}1)\}\bigr) \ge\delta,$$ where $\mathbb R^2$ is identified with $\mathbb C$.* 2. *If $c$ is a Lebesgue-measurable five-coloring with two linearly independent periods, then $\mathbb P(c(A)=c(A+U))\ge\delta$, where $A$ is uniform in a fundamental parallelogram and $U$ is an independent uniform unit direction. For any Lebesgue-measurable five-coloring, if the conditional square-table frequencies $$\mathbb P\bigl(c(A_R)=c(A_R+U)\mid A_R+U\in[-R,R]^2\bigr) \qquad(R>1)$$ converge as $R\to\infty$, their limit is at least $\delta$; here $A_R$ is uniform in $[-R,R]^2$ and independent of $U$.
  - Explanation: A common positive constant bounds monochromatic unit-pair frequency from below for invariant means, periodic measurable colorings, and convergent conditional square-table frequencies. The proof obtains the constant from an existential finite obstruction and does not display that obstruction.
- **claim-wild-law-haar-rigidity** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-rigidity)): Every $K$-invariant probability measure $\nu$ on $D$ with $\nu(C)=0$ is Haar measure. Equivalently, writing $m_D$ for normalized Haar measure, $$\label{eq:source-3} \nu\text{ is wild}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\nu=m_D.$$
  - Explanation: Rotation invariance together with exclusion of ordinary continuous characters uniquely determines Haar probability on the compact character group. Its nonzero algebraic-translation Fourier coefficients therefore vanish.
- **claim-wild-null-cosets-and-lines** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-null-events)): Let $\nu$ be wild. Then every coset of $C$ has $\nu$-measure zero, and the law of the difference of two independent $\nu$-samples gives $C$ measure zero. Moreover, for each $a\in E\setminus\{0\}$, the event $$L_a=\{d\in D:t\mapsto d(at)\text{ is continuous on }F \text{ in its usual topology}\}$$ is Borel and has $\nu$-measure zero.
  - Explanation: A wild law charges no continuous-character coset and no event of continuity along a nonzero algebraic line. Independent differences also avoid the continuous-character subgroup.
- **claim-wild-vanishing-line-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-line-average)): If $\nu$ is wild, then $$\label{eq:source-2} I\bigl(\phi(t)f_\nu(at)\bigr)=0 \qquad\bigl(a\in E\setminus\{0\},\ \phi\in C_c(\mathbb R)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The invariant integral annihilates a wild Fourier coefficient along every nonzero algebraic line after multiplication by a compactly supported continuous test function. Ordinary measurability of the restricted coefficient is unnecessary.
- **claim-equivariant-probability-center** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-location)): For every Borel probability measure $\lambda$ on $\mathbb R^2$, the function $$\label{eq:spectral-location-objective} J_\lambda(s)=\int_{\mathbb R^2} \left(\sqrt{1+\|s-t\|^2}-\sqrt{1+\|t\|^2}\right)\,d\lambda(t)$$ has a unique minimizer $L(\lambda)$. The map $\lambda\mapsto L(\lambda)$ is Borel for the usual Borel structure on the space of probability measures. It is equivariant under translations and orthogonal maps.
  - Explanation: A bounded-difference objective selects a unique center for any Borel probability on the plane, without a moment assumption. The selector is Borel and respects translations and orthogonal maps.
- **claim-relative-wild-singularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-singularity)): Let $X$ be an ergodic standard probability-preserving $K$-system, and let $d:X\to D$ be an equivariant measurable map with wild law. For every countable tower of factors as above, and every factor $Y$ in that tower, the law $\nu_Y$ of $$d(x)-d(x')$$ for two conditionally independent samples of $X$ over $Y$ satisfies $\nu_Y(C)=0$.
  - Explanation: Conditional independence over any factor in the specified compact tower preserves exclusion of continuous characters for the difference law. The tower approximation property and the wild equivariant marginal are essential inputs.
- **claim-countable-compact-tower** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-tower)): Let $X$ be an ergodic standard probability-preserving $K$-system. There is a countable ordinal $\eta$ and an increasing tower of factors $(Y_\alpha)_{\alpha\leq\eta}$ with the following properties:* 1. *$Y_0$ is the trivial factor, and each limit stage is the factor generated by its predecessors;* 2. *every successor extension $Y_{\alpha+1}\to Y_\alpha$ satisfies <a href="#eq:source-4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-4">[eq:source-4]</a>;* 3. *for $Y=Y_\eta$, the system $X\times_YX$ is ergodic.
  - Explanation: An ergodic standard system admits a countable compact-factor tower ending at a factor whose relative self-product is ergodic. Separability forces the transfinite enlargement procedure to stop at a countable stage.
- **claim-conditional-multiple-average-decoupling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-multiple-averages)): Suppose that $X$ is an ergodic standard probability-preserving $K$-system, that $Y$ is an invariant factor, and that $X\times_YX$ is ergodic. Let $P=\mathbb E(\,\cdot\mid Y)$. For pairwise distinct integers $n_1,\ldots,n_l$ and bounded functions $v_1,\ldots,v_l$ on $X$, $$\label{eq:source-6} \operatorname{Avg}_{u\in\Phi_N} \left\\|P\left(\prod_{j=1}^lU_{u^{n_j}}v_j\right) -\prod_{j=1}^lU_{u^{n_j}}Pv_j\right\\|_2^2 \longrightarrow0$$ along every Følner sequence $(\Phi_N)$ in $K$. The pair criterion used in the proof is, for bounded $v,w$ and $d\ne0$, $$\label{eq:source-7} \operatorname{Avg}_{u\in\Phi_N} \lVert P(vU_{u^d}w)-Pv\,U_{u^d}Pw\rVert_2^2\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Products of distinct rotation powers asymptotically decouple under conditional expectation along every Følner sequence when the relative product is ergodic. The pair criterion supplies the centered induction used for the multiple-product conclusion.
- **claim-radial-difference-and-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-radial)): For $w(r)=-\log m(r)\in[0,\infty]$, with $-\log0=\infty$, one has $$\label{eq:source-8} w(\|A-B\|)\leq w(A)+w(B)\qquad(A,B\in F_{\geq0}).$$ For every $r\in F$ with $r>0$ and every Følner sequence in $K$, $$\label{eq:source-9} \operatorname{Avg}_{u\in\Phi_N}m(2r\|\operatorname{Re}u\|) \longrightarrow m(r)^2.$$
  - Explanation: The logarithmic radial coefficient obeys a difference inequality, including infinite values. A second identity relates its radial rotation average to the squared coefficient at the starting radius.
- **claim-number-field-general-position** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-general-position)): Let $N_0\subset F$ be a number field, let $N=N_0(i)$, and let $d_0=[N:\mathbb Q]$. There are arbitrarily large finite sets $S\subset K\cap N$ such that every $d_0$ distinct elements of $S$ form a $\mathbb Q$-basis of $N$.
  - Explanation: Algebraic unit rotations can be chosen in arbitrarily large finite collections such that every subcollection of the field dimension forms a rational basis. The proof avoids finitely many proper rational subspaces using rational-function coordinates.
- **claim-capped-integer-residue-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-capped-residues)): Let $w:F_{\geq0}\to[0,\infty]$ satisfy $w(0)=0$ and <a href="#eq:source-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-8">[eq:source-8]</a>. Let $p_1,\ldots,p_q\in F_{\geq0}$ with $p=\max_jp_j>0$ and $w(p_j)\leq H<\infty$. If $$z=\sum_{j=1}^q a_jp_j\in[0,p),\qquad a_j\in\mathbb Z,\qquad\sum_j\|a_j\|\leq M,$$ then $w(z)\leq4MH$.
  - Explanation: The difference inequality controls bounded-length signed integer combinations after keeping partial sums under the common cap. The bound applies when the final combination lies in the specified residue interval.
- **claim-continuous-spectral-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-palettes-continuous-factor)): Let a standard probability space carry a measure-preserving action of the additive group $E$, with Koopman operators $T_z$. The subspace $$\mathcal H_c =\left\{v\in L^2: \lim_{\substack{z\to0\\z\in E}}\lVert T_zv-v\rVert_2=0\right\}$$ is $L^2$ of a factor. It is also the spectral subspace on $C$ for the representation of $E_{\rm disc}$. The operators on this subspace extend to a strongly continuous unitary representation $T_x$, $x\in\mathbb R^2$, which preserves the positive cone and the constant one.* *If the system also has rotations $V_u$, $u\in K$, satisfying $V_uT_zV_u^{-1}=T_{uz}$, then $\mathcal H_c$ and its orthogonal projection are rotation invariant.
  - Explanation: The translation-continuous vectors form the functions of a factor and coincide with the continuous-character spectral subspace. Projection is therefore conditional expectation, preserving positivity and constants, and the factor translations extend strongly continuously to the real plane.
- **claim-forward-coloring-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-palettes-forward)): Let $k$ be a positive integer. If the plane admits an unrestricted proper $k$-coloring, then it admits a weak measurable $k$-coloring.
  - Explanation: An unrestricted proper coloring yields an invariant labeling law. Removing the wild spectral remainder through the continuous factor preserves zero unit correlations, and a measurable sample supplies a weak coloring.
- **claim-density-point-unit-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-palettes-density-proper)): In a weak measurable $k$-coloring with classes $A_1,\ldots,A_k$, the full-density-point set of each $A_i$ contains no unit pair. These density-point sets are disjoint and their union is conull.
  - Explanation: Density-one points of a weak color class form a strictly unit-independent set. These disjoint sets cover a conull subset of the plane, enabling the converse transfer through finite configurations and compactness.
- **claim-palette-transfer-exclusions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-palette-transfer)): The following assertions hold at every fixed center $x$.* 1. *For almost every independent-angle pair $(e,v)\in S\times S$, $$\label{eq:source-10} c(x+e+v)\notin P_x(e)\cup P_x(v).$$* 2. *If $e$ is an angular density-one point of $D_{x,i}=\{e':i\in P_x(e')\}$, then for each $v$ nonparallel to $e$, the spatial density of $A_i$ at $x+e+v$ is zero.* 3. *Let $z$ be any fixed center, possibly $z=x$. Suppose on an open angular arc, or locally on a union of such arcs, there is a smooth representation $$z+f=x+s(f)+t(f),\qquad s(f),t(f)\in S,$$ with $s(f),t(f)$ nonparallel and $s$ a local diffeomorphism in the angular coordinate. Then almost everywhere on that domain, $$\label{eq:source-11} P_z(f)\cap P_x(s(f))=\varnothing.$$* *Each assertion permits an exceptional angular set depending on the fixed centers and on the specified maps.
  - Explanation: Palette labels are excluded at almost every sum of independent directions. Angular density transfers to spatial density exclusion at every nonparallel second direction, and smooth transport yields disjoint palettes at fixed centers under the stated nonsingularity conditions.
- **claim-sixty-degree-palette-disjointness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-palettes-sixty-degree)): At every fixed center $x$, $$\label{eq:source-12} P_x(f)\cap P_x(Rf)=\varnothing \qquad\text{for almost every }f\in S.$$
  - Explanation: The equilateral sum relation makes palettes at successive sixty-degree directions disjoint almost everywhere at each fixed center.
- **claim-binary-centers-uniform-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-separation)): There is an absolute constant $\delta_*>0$ such that, for every fixed pair of labels, any two distinct centers binary for that pair are at distance at least $\delta_*$.
  - Explanation: Centers whose palettes meet the same fixed pair of labels are uniformly separated. Smooth transport produces circle maps preserving the binary state; expansion and bounded distortion contradict its required angular alternation when centers are too close.
- **claim-palette-size-at-most-two** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-palette-size)): For every center $x\in\mathbb R^2$, $$\label{eq:source-13} 1\le\lvert P_x(e)\rvert\le2 \qquad\text{for almost every }e\in S.$$ The angular exceptional set may depend on $x$.
  - Explanation: Every fixed center has a nonempty palette of at most two labels almost everywhere in direction. A positive-measure triple palette would create infinitely many binary centers for one complementary pair on a compact circle, contradicting uniform separation.
- **claim-transition-edge-avoiding-trace** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-avoiding-trace)): If $ij\in\Gamma_x$, there is a trace $b^{ij}\in\mathcal W_x$ with $b^{ij}_i=b^{ij}_j=0$ almost everywhere. Consequently, $$P_x(e)\not\subset\{i,j\} \quad\text{for almost every }e\in S, \qquad ij\in\Gamma_x. \label{eq:source-15}$$
  - Explanation: An abundant transition edge gives a limiting circle trace avoiding both endpoint labels. Consequently, the maximal palette cannot be contained entirely in that edge's label pair.
- **claim-state-support-strict-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-strict-separation)): Suppose $A,B\subset S$ are states at $x$ representing disjoint label groups whose union is $\mathcal I\setminus\{X\}$, for a fixed label $X$. At any nonparallel density-one directions $a$ of $A$ and $b$ of $B$, the point $x+a+b$ has spatial density one of $X$.* *If $\sigma(A)>0$, the closed essential support of $B$ contains no pair at chordal distance one. In particular, it has a strict angular separation from its image under $R$ and under $R^{-1}$, with the empty-support case interpreted vacuously.* *The separation is uniform along a sequence of centers and states $(x_n,A_n,B_n)$ with the same represented groups, provided $\mathbf 1_{A_n}\to\mathbf 1_A$ in $L^1(S)$ for a set $A$ of positive measure. More precisely, for all sufficiently large $n$, angular differences between two points of $\operatorname{ess\,supp}_S B_n$ stay a fixed positive distance from both $\kappa$ and $-\kappa$ modulo $2\pi$.
  - Explanation: Nonparallel density directions representing four labels force density one of the remaining label at their sum. Properness of those density points excludes unit chords in the opposite state support, with a uniform margin under the stated state convergence.
- **claim-singleton-pair-alternation-impossible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-two-state)): At a fixed center, there is no measurable state function with only the two values $X,D$, representing respectively a singleton $\{X\}$ and a fixed disjoint pair $D$, and changing state under $R$ almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: A singleton state and a disjoint pair state cannot alternate measurably under the sixty-degree rotation. The proof uses an irrational angular shift to force a unit equilateral triangle into the remaining two labels.
- **claim-cyclic-centers-closed-locally-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cyclic-local-finiteness)): The set $$\mathcal L=\{x\in\mathbb R^2:\Gamma_x\text{ contains a cycle}\} \label{eq:source-19}$$ is closed and locally finite: it has finite intersection with every compact subset of the plane.
  - Explanation: The centers with a cyclic transition graph form a closed locally finite set. Triangle loci use binary separation; four-cycle and five-cycle loci require separate angular support arguments to rule out accumulation.
- **claim-four-cycle-state-continuity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-four-continuity)): For each $s\in\{X,U,V\}$, the map $x\mapsto\mathbf 1_{\{w_x=s\}}$ is continuous from $L$ into $L^1(S)$.
  - Explanation: Canonical singleton and diagonal state indicators vary continuously in angular integral norm along a fixed four-cycle locus. Avoiding traces identify weak limits as the canonical limiting states, upgrading indicator convergence to strong convergence.
- **claim-support-contact-global-step-ban** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-step-ban)): If $i,i+1\in S(a)$ at any direction $a$, then the transitions $i\to i+1$ and $i+1\to i$ under $R$ are absent almost everywhere on the entire angular circle at $x$.
  - Explanation: A support contact between adjacent diagonal states prohibits both corresponding angular transitions everywhere up to angular null sets. The proof uses separate avoiding traces at the translated center and sixty-degree exclusion there.
- **claim-common-exclusion-interface-cycle** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-interface-cycle)): For the weak measurable five-coloring under consideration, there exist an integer $\ell\in\{3,4,5\}$, distinct labels $i_0,\ldots,i_{\ell-1}\in\mathcal I$, a point $x\in\mathbb R^2$, and a radius $\rho>0$ with the following properties. For each edge $i_ji_{j+1}$ of the simple label cycle, with indices modulo $\ell$, there are a compact connected set $K_j\subset\overline{B(x,\rho)}$, a direction $v_j\in S$, and a sequence $q_{j,k}\in K_j\setminus\{x\}$ such that* 1. *$x\in K_j$ and $K_j\cap\partial B(x,\rho)\ne\varnothing$;* 2. *$q_{j,k}\to x$ and $(q_{j,k}-x)/\|q_{j,k}-x\|\to v_j$;* 3. *both endpoint supports are excluded from the same open set: $$H_{i_j}\cap\Delta(K_j)= H_{i_{j+1}}\cap\Delta(K_j)=\varnothing.$$* *The extracted cycle need not consist of edges of $\Gamma_x$.
  - Explanation: The assumed weak five-coloring supplies a simple label cycle and compact connected sets attached to its edges. They share a center, reach an outer boundary, and exclude both endpoint supports from their open straddling regions. The extracted cycle is not asserted to be a cycle in the center's transition graph.
- **claim-disk-average-unit-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-disk-averages)): For every pair $q,z\in\mathbb R^2$ with $\|q-z\|=1$ and every label $i$, $$p_i^\epsilon(q)+p_i^\epsilon(z)\le1. \label{eq:source-17}$$ If $q_n\to q$, $\epsilon_n\to0$, and $\liminf_n p_i^{\epsilon_n}(q_n)>0$, then $q\in H_i$.
  - Explanation: Disk averages satisfy a pointwise same-label sum bound at every unit pair, without exceptional unit pairs. A positive limiting local average also detects membership in the closed spatial essential support.
- **claim-missing-edge-small-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transitions-missing-edge)): If $ij\notin\Gamma_x$, there is a ball $B$ about $x$ such that, for every smaller ball $B'$ with $\overline{B'}\subset B$, $$\int_{B'}p_i^\epsilon(q)p_j^\epsilon(q)\,dq=o(\epsilon). \label{eq:source-18}$$ For any ball $B''$ with $\overline{B''}\subset B'$ and any fixed $\alpha>0$, the set $$\{q\in B'':p_i^\epsilon(q)>\alpha,\quad p_j^\epsilon(q)>\alpha\}$$ can be covered by finitely many open disks whose total radii tend to zero as $\epsilon\downarrow0$. Every point of the set lies in a disk interior.
  - Explanation: Absence of a transition edge gives overlap smaller than the smoothing scale on smaller balls. Fixed-threshold violations can then be covered by finitely many open disks with vanishing total radii.
- **claim-small-holes-tree-fillings** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-interfaces-hole-maps)): There are $\epsilon_n\downarrow0$ and finite families $\mathcal H_n$ of open disks with pairwise disjoint closures such that their total radii tend to zero and the following hold for all sufficiently large $n$.* 1. *For each $x\in L_0$, precisely one disk of $\mathcal H_n$ is designated the core disk $B_{n,x}$ and contains $x$ in its interior. Different points give different core disks. If $x\in Q$, then $\overline{B_{n,x}}\subset\operatorname{int}(Q)$; if $x\notin Q$, then $\overline{B_{n,x}}\cap Q=\varnothing$.* 2. *The map $q^{\epsilon_n}$ takes values in $\mathscr G$ on $Q\setminus\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal H_n}B$.* 3. *There is a continuous map $$g_n:Q\setminus\bigcup_{x\in L_0\cap Q}B_{n,x} \longrightarrow\mathscr G \label{eq:source-23}$$ equal to $q^{\epsilon_n}$ outside all the open disks of $\mathcal H_n$. It is obtained by filling the non-core disks through trees, preserving their boundary values.
  - Explanation: Threshold violations are enclosed in disjoint small holes. Outside them the probability map is graph-valued, and non-core holes can be filled through trees while retaining the original boundary values. Only disks around cyclic centers remain removed.
- **claim-square-cover-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interfaces-cover)): A closed square of side ten has no relatively open cover of multiplicity at most two and mesh at most two, where mesh is the supremum of the Euclidean diameters of its members.
  - Explanation: A square of the stated size cannot admit an open cover simultaneously having the prescribed small mesh and low multiplicity. A partition of unity would otherwise give a near-identity map whose image lies in finitely many segments, contradicting the planar fixed-point argument.
- **claim-non-null-core-boundary-loop** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interfaces-core-loop)): For all sufficiently large $n$, some core disk $B_{n,x}$ contained in $Q$ has a boundary loop whose image under $g_n$ is not null homotopic in $\mathscr G$.
  - Explanation: At least one core boundary loop is non-null for all sufficiently advanced approximations. Filling every core would yield a graph-valued map on the entire square and an open component cover forbidden by the cover obstruction.
- **claim-reduced-graph-circuit-midpoint-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interfaces-graph-word)): Let $G$ be a finite connected simple graph. Every nontrivial free homotopy class of loops in $G$ has a nonempty cyclically reduced closed edge word $W$. Such a word contains a simple cycle. Moreover, if $m$ is the midpoint of any edge occurring in $W$, every loop freely homotopic to a nonzero power of $W$ meets $m$.
  - Explanation: A nontrivial graph loop has a nonempty cyclically reduced circuit containing a simple cycle. Every loop in the free homotopy class of a nonzero power must meet the midpoint of every edge occurring in that circuit.
- **claim-annular-connected-midpoint-crossing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interfaces-annular-crossing)): Let $B$ be an open disk containing $x$, with $\overline B\subset B(x,\rho)$, and set $\mathcal A=\overline{B(x,\rho)}\setminus B$. Suppose $g:\mathcal A\to G$ is continuous, where $G$ is a finite connected simple graph, and the image of the counterclockwise inner boundary has nonempty cyclically reduced word $W$. If $m$ is the midpoint of an edge occurring in $W$, then $g^{-1}(\{m\})$ has a compact connected component meeting both boundary circles of $\mathcal A$.
  - Explanation: An edge midpoint used by the nontrivial inner-boundary circuit has a connected preimage crossing the closed annulus. Otherwise a separating polygonal loop with nonzero winding could avoid the midpoint while representing a nonzero circuit power.
- **claim-one-sided-angular-strip-restrictions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-strips)): Both $O(e)$ and $I(e)$ are nonempty for every $e\notin\mathcal B$. On an angular neighborhood of $e$, an open strip just outside radius one uses only $O(e)$, and an open strip just inside radius one uses only $I(e)$, in both cases up to plane-null sets. Moreover, $$\label{eq:source-25} P_x(e)\cap O(e)\ne\varnothing, \qquad P_x(e)\cap I(e)\ne\varnothing \quad\text{for almost every }e\in S\setminus\mathcal B.$$
  - Explanation: Each generic direction has nonempty inner and outer allowed-label sets. The connected exclusions produce open one-sided strips using only those sets, and separate limiting traces ensure that the palette meets each set almost everywhere.
- **claim-interface-five-cycle-impossible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-five)): The extracted interface cycle cannot have length five.
  - Explanation: A five-cycle gives antipodally identical palette options. Successive disjoint diagonals would force all six rotation steps to have the same sign, which cannot close modulo five.
- **claim-four-cycle-word-parity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-words)): Every word $w(t)$ contains $X$, and all its $X$ positions have the same parity. Every direction outside $\mathcal B$ is split.
  - Explanation: Each four-cycle angular word contains the outsider on a single parity. The other positions and their antipodes force every direction away from the finite perpendicular set to have a split sign pattern.
- **claim-four-split-rays-projective-arcs** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-four-rays)): Under the conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:angular-words" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:angular-words">44</a>, the four rays consist of two distinct antipodal pairs. Opposite directions occupy opposite edge positions. On the projective angular circle of circumference $\pi$, the forced diagonal $d$ equals $U$ on one open arc and $V$ on its complementary open arc. One of these arcs has length at most $\pi/2$.
  - Explanation: The split-ray condition forces two distinct antipodal pairs with opposite edge positions. Their perpendicular boundaries divide the projective circle into complementary arcs carrying the two forced diagonals, with a short arc among them.
- **claim-interface-four-cycle-impossible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-four)): The extracted interface cycle cannot have length four.
  - Explanation: Changing outsider parity between nearby words would require both forced-diagonal arcs to contain three equally spaced projective directions. The short-arc bound prevents that, so parity would be constant despite being reversed by the rotation.
- **claim-triangle-local-binary-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-binary-local)): If $e_0,Re_0\notin\mathcal B$, then $s$ is almost everywhere constant on an angular neighborhood of $e_0$.
  - Explanation: Two outsider-only strips at successive directions allow even unit-edge paths between nearby points. Typical-point properness makes the strip color locally constant, and radial traces transfer that constancy to the outsider angular state.
- **claim-triangle-boundary-single-rotation-orbit** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-boundary)): The essential boundary of $s$ consists of exactly one six-position $R$-orbit. Its complementary sectors have angle $\pi/3$, with alternating essential values $X,Y$.
  - Explanation: The outsider state has exactly one essential-boundary rotation orbit. Its complementary sectors alternate between the outsiders; the finite set of perpendicular ray classes prevents a second boundary orbit.
- **claim-polynomial-three-label-region** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-region)): Put $z=\xi+i\upsilon$, $l=\xi^2+\upsilon^2$. If $0<l<4$ and $$\label{eq:source-27} P:=\upsilon^2(3\xi^2-\upsilon^2)^2 <P':=l^3(1-l/4)(l-1)^2,$$ then a neighborhood of $x+z$ uses only the three triangle labels up to a plane-null set.
  - Explanation: The strict polynomial inequality ensures that the two unit-direction summands lie inside opposite outsider sectors. Smooth transport then excludes both outsiders from an open neighborhood of the sum.
- **claim-seven-point-placement-certificate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-certificate)): Every $z_g$ in <a href="#eq:angular-placement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:angular-placement">[eq:angular-placement]</a> satisfies $0<\lvert z_g\rvert^2<4$ and <a href="#eq:source-27" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-27">[eq:source-27]</a>.
  - Explanation: Every explicitly placed Moser vertex satisfies the strict squared-radius and polynomial conditions. Rational coordinate boxes and rational product bounds establish membership without replacing the strict inequalities by numerical approximations.
- **claim-interface-triangle-impossible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-three)): The extracted interface cycle cannot have length three.
  - Explanation: A common small translation places every certified Moser vertex at a typical point using only the triangle labels. The preserved unit edges then give a proper three-coloring of a graph whose overlapping triangles forbid it.

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-weak-coloring",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a positive integer, let $S^1\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be the unit circle, and let $\\sigma$ be its normalized arc-length measure.",
          "source_id": "PM-158.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-weak-coloring",
          "exact_quote": "A *weak measurable $k$-coloring* is a Lebesgue measurable function $c:\\mathbb R^2\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, with color classes $A_i=c^{-1}(\\{i\\})$, such that, for every $R>0$, $$\\sum_{i=1}^k\\int_{B(0,R)}\\int_{S^1}\n  \\mathbf 1_{A_i}(x)\\mathbf 1_{A_i}(x+u)\\,d\\sigma(u)\\,dx=0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-158.1"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Consider a tower $(Y_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\le\\theta}$ of factors of an ergodic system $X$, where $\\theta$ is a countable ordinal.         }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-palettes-continuous-factor",
        "exact_quote": "Let a standard probability space carry a measure-preserving action of the additive group $E$, with Koopman operators $T_z$. The subspace $$\\mathcal H_c\n =\\left\\{v\\in L^2:\n       \\lim_{\\substack{z\\to0\\\\z\\in E}}\\lVert T_zv-v\\rVert_2=0\\right\\}$$ is $L^2$ of a factor. It is also the spectral subspace on $C$ for the representation of $E_{\\rm disc}$. The operators on this subspace extend to a strongly continuous unitary representation $T_x$, $x\\in\\mathbb R^2$, which preserves the positive cone and the constant one.*\n\n*If the system also has rotations $V_u$, $u\\in K$, satisfying $V_uT_zV_u^{-1}=T_{uz}$, then $\\mathcal H_c$ and its orthogonal projection are rotation invariant.",
        "source_id": "PM-158.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $A,B\\subset S$ are states at $x$ representing disjoint label groups whose union is $\\mathcal I\\setminus\\{X\\}$, for a fixed label $X$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Weak measurable coloring** ([source](#stmt-def-weak-coloring)): Let $k$ be a positive integer, let $S^1\subset\mathbb R^2$ be the unit circle, and let $\sigma$ be its normalized arc-length measure. A *weak measurable $k$-coloring* is a Lebesgue measurable function $c:\mathbb R^2\to\{1,\ldots,k\}$, with color classes $A_i=c^{-1}(\{i\})$, such that, for every $R>0$, $$\sum_{i=1}^k\int_{B(0,R)}\int_{S^1} \mathbf 1_{A_i}(x)\mathbf 1_{A_i}(x+u)\,d\sigma(u)\,dx=0.$$ The integrals use the completed product measure; equivalently, one may first choose Borel representatives of the color classes forming a partition of the plane.
- **Algebraic plane, algebraic rotations, character group, and continuous characters** ([source](#stmt-def-spectral-groups)): Identify the plane with $\mathbb C$, and set $$F=\overline{\mathbb Q}\cap\mathbb R,\qquad E=F(i),\qquad K=\{u\in E:u\bar u=1\}.$$ We regard $E$ as an additive discrete group and $K$ as a multiplicative discrete group. Let $$D=\widehat{E_{\mathrm{disc}}}$$ be the compact character group of $E$. A point $d\in D$ is a homomorphism from the additive group $E$ to the unit complex group $\mathbb T$. We use additive notation on $D$, so $(d+d')(z)=d(z)d'(z)$. For $a,z\in E$, put $$(ad)(z)=d(az),\qquad \psi_z(d)=d(z).$$ The subgroup of ordinary continuous characters is $$\label{eq:spectral-continuous-characters} C=j(\mathbb R^2),\qquad j(\xi)(z)=\exp(i\xi\cdot z),$$ where the dot product uses the real coordinates of $z$.
- **Wild character law and its Fourier coefficients** ([source](#stmt-def-spectral-wild)): A $K$-invariant Borel probability measure $\nu$ on $D$ is called *wild* if $\nu(C)=0$. Its Fourier coefficients are denoted by $$f_\nu(z)=\int_D\psi_z(d)\,d\nu(d),\qquad z\in E.$$
- **Circle traces and maximal palettes** ([source](#stmt-def-palettes-traces)): For each fixed center $x$, let $\mathcal W_x$ be the family of vector functions in $L^\infty(S;\mathbb R^5)$ that can be obtained as $$b=\operatorname{w}^*\!-\!\lim_{n\to\infty} (a_i(y_n+r_ne))_{i=1}^5,\qquad y_n\to x,\quad r_n\to1,$$ where every $(y_n,r_n)$ is a typical circle sampling. Constant sequences are permitted. For a label $i$, form the essential measurable union $$D_{x,i}=\mathop{\operatorname{ess\,union}}_{b\in\mathcal W_x} \{e:b_i(e)>0\}.$$ The maximal palette is the measurable set-valued function $$P_x(e)=\{i:e\in D_{x,i}\},$$ defined up to angular null sets.
- **Transition graph** ([source](#stmt-def-transitions-graph)): For $x\in\mathbb R^2$, let $\Gamma_x$ be the simple graph on $\mathcal I$ in which $ij$ is an edge precisely when, for every ball $B$ centered at $x$ with positive finite radius, $$\limsup_{\substack{h\to0\\h\ne0}} \frac{D_{ij}(B,h)}{\|h\|}>0. \label{eq:source-14}$$
- **Proper coloring and chromatic number of the plane** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A proper $k$-coloring of the Euclidean plane is a function $c:\mathbb R^2\to\{1,\ldots,k\}$ such that $c(x)\ne c(y)$ whenever $\lVert x-y\rVert=1$. The Hadwiger–Nelson problem asks for the least such $k$, denoted by $\chi(\mathbb R^2)$, with no regularity imposed on the color classes.
- **Invariant integral along algebraic lines** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a bounded function $h:F\to\mathbb C$ supported in a bounded interval, define $$\label{eq:spectral-invariant-integral} I(h)=\mathfrak m\left(t+\mathbb Z\longmapsto \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}h(t+k)\right).$$
- **Continuous translation vectors** ([source](#stmt-lem-palettes-continuous-factor)): The subspace $$\mathcal H_c =\left\{v\in L^2: \lim_{\substack{z\to0\\z\in E}}\lVert T_zv-v\rVert_2=0\right\}$$ is $L^2$ of a factor.
- **Typical point** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Call a point *typical* if it has density one of its actual color.
- **Closed spatial essential support** ([source](#pm-h-28)): For each label let $$H_i=\{q\in\mathbb R^2: \lvert A_i\cap B(q,r)\rvert>0\text{ for every }r>0\}$$ be its closed spatial essential support.
- **Typical circle sampling** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Call $(y,r)$, with $y\in\mathbb R^2$ and $r>0$, a *typical circle sampling* if $y+re$ is typical for almost every $e\in S$.
- **Binary center for a fixed label pair** ([source](#subsec:palettes-binary)): Call a center $x$ *binary for $\{i_+,i_-\}$* if $$P_x(e)\cap\{i_+,i_-\}\ne\varnothing \qquad\text{for almost every }e\in S.$$
- **Angular state** ([source](#pm-h-34)): A *state* at a center $x$ will mean a measurable angular set together with a specified nonempty group of labels contained in $P_x(e)$ almost everywhere on that set.
- **Angular essential support** ([source](#pm-h-34)): For a measurable set $A\subset S$, its closed essential support is $$\operatorname{ess\,supp}_S A =\{a\in S:\sigma(A\cap I)>0\text{ for every open arc }I\ni a\}.$$
- **Cyclic-center locus** ([source](#stmt-thm-cyclic-local-finiteness)): The set $$\mathcal L=\{x\in\mathbb R^2:\Gamma_x\text{ contains a cycle}\} \label{eq:source-19}$$ is closed and locally finite: it has finite intersection with every compact subset of the plane.
- **Open straddling region** ([source](#sec:interfaces)): For a nonempty compact set $K\subset\mathbb R^2$, define its open straddling region by $$\Delta(K)= \left\{z\in\mathbb R^2: \min_{q\in K}\|z-q\|<1<\max_{q\in K}\|z-q\| \right\}. \label{eq:source-24}$$
- **Disk-average probability coordinates** ([source](#pm-h-41)): For $\epsilon>0$, set $$p_i^\epsilon(q)=\frac1{\pi\epsilon^2} \int_{B(0,\epsilon)}a_i(q+v)\,dv.$$
- **Thresholded probability map** ([source](#eq:source-22)): Fix $0<\alpha<1/5$, and define $$q_i^\epsilon(z)= \frac{(p_i^\epsilon(z)-\alpha)_+} {\sum_{k\in\mathcal I}(p_k^\epsilon(z)-\alpha)_+}, \qquad i\in\mathcal I. \label{eq:source-22}$$
- **Complete label graph** ([source](#pm-h-42)): Let $\mathscr G$ be the one-skeleton of that simplex, realized as the complete graph on its five vertices.
- **Moser seven-vertex configuration** ([source](#eq:angular-seven-vectors)): The following coordinates realize the seven-vertex obstruction of Moser and Moser (1961) [[MoserMoser1961](#ref-MoserMoser1961)], a unit-distance graph that cannot be colored with three labels: $$\label{eq:angular-seven-vectors} \mathcal G=\{0,A,B,T,uA,uB,uT\},\qquad A=\frac{\sqrt3+i}{2},\quad B=\frac{\sqrt3-i}{2},\quad T=\sqrt3,\quad u=\frac{5+i\sqrt{11}}6.$$
- **Rigid placement of the Moser configuration** ([source](#eq:angular-placement)): We place this graph using $$\label{eq:angular-placement} z_g=\frac{35+12i}{37} \left(g+\frac{-290+149i}{250}\right), \qquad g\in\mathcal G.$$

          "anchor": "eq:interfaces-annulus-area",
          "exact_quote": "We will repeatedly use the following area estimate. For a closed disk $\\overline{B(u,r)}$ with $r<1$, every point at distance one from some point of that disk belongs to $$E(u,r)=\\{z:1-r\\le |z-u|\\le1+r\\}.$$ The triangle inequality proves this containment, and the annulus has area $4\\pi r$. For the families in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:interfaces-hole-maps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:interfaces-hole-maps\">37</a>, write $$E_n=\\bigcup_{B(u,r)\\in\\mathcal H_n}E(u,r),\n \\qquad\n |E_n|\\le4\\pi\\sum_{B(u,r)\\in\\mathcal H_n}r\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:interfaces-annulus-area}$$ Here all radii are less than one for large $n$. If $z\\notin E_n$, no exact unit-distance point from $z$ lies in any closed hole disk, regardless of how many disks there are.",
          "source_id": "PM-158.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-wild-null-events** [claim](#stmt-lem-spectral-null-events) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spectral-null-events): Use divisibility of algebraic rotations to rule out finite nontrivial coset orbits, then compare continuity events on nonparallel lines. -> Null cosets, wild independent differences, and null line-continuity events.
- **step-vanishing-line-average** [claim](#stmt-lem-line-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-line-average): Show that line representations have no continuous vectors, then construct a continuous vector by invariant integration and force it to vanish. -> Vanishing invariant line averages of wild Fourier coefficients.
- **step-equivariant-center** [claim](#stmt-lem-spectral-location) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spectral-location): Combine bounded objective differences, strict convexity, and coercivity; select rational approximate minimizers to prove Borel dependence. -> Unique measurable center with translation and orthogonal equivariance.
- **step-relative-singularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-singularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-singularity): Use universal transfinite induction; convert positive conditional coset mass into finitely many equivariant centers and contradict conditional phase-collision bounds with compact approximation. -> Wild conditional difference laws over every tower factor.
- **step-compact-factor-tower** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-tower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-tower): Extract finite-rank equivariant modules from invariant relative kernels and iterate factor enlargement until separability forces termination. -> Countable compact tower with ergodic terminal relative product.
- **step-conditional-multiple-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-multiple-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-multiple-averages): Prove the pair criterion on the relative product, extend it to tensors, and apply centered induction with a Hilbert-space correlation estimate along every Følner sequence. -> Asymptotic decoupling of projected multiple products.
- **step-radial-identities** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-radial): Apply conditional decoupling to equal-modulus algebraic identities and take negative logarithms of the resulting multiplicative inequality. -> Logarithmic difference inequality and radial rotation-average identity.
- **step-number-field-general-position** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-general-position) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-general-position): Parametrize algebraic rotations rationally and inductively avoid the finitely many rational spans of earlier selections. -> Arbitrarily large finite rotation sets in rational linear general position.
- **step-capped-residue-addition** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-capped-residues) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-capped-residues): Maintain every signed partial sum as a residue under the maximum input and control each update using the difference inequality. -> The stated uniform weight bound for a final residue.
- **step-haar-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-thm-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-capped-residues-2): Turn a positive radial coefficient into a syndetic rotation set, extract finitely many number-field bases, and obtain a positive interval lower bound contradicting the invariant line average; finish by ergodic decomposition. -> Every wild invariant probability equals Haar probability.
- **step-continuous-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-palettes-continuous-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palettes-continuous-factor): Identify continuous vectors spectrally, show bounded continuous vectors form an algebra generating a factor, and extend translations by strong limits. -> Positive conditional projection and strongly continuous real-plane translations.
- **step-forward-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-palettes-forward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-palettes-forward): Average proper algebraic labelings into an invariant law, project label indicators to the continuous factor, remove zero-correlated wild remainders, and select one jointly measurable sample. -> A weak measurable coloring with the same color count.
- **step-density-point-properness** [claim](#stmt-lem-palettes-density-proper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palettes-density-proper): Perturb a hypothetical same-class density-one unit pair using a disk and angular arc to obtain positive product-measure badness. -> Disjoint conull density classes containing no unit pair.
- **step-colorability-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-thm-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palettes-density-proper-2): Translate each finite configuration into the conull density classes, then apply the finite intersection property in the full labeling product. -> Proper and weak measurable colorability are equivalent.
- **step-palette-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-palette-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palette-transfer): Combine strong convergence of the second circle-sum factor with weak-star trace convergence, then use local inverse maps and angular density to transport exclusions. -> Independent-angle, density, and smooth-transport palette exclusions.
- **step-sixty-degree-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-cor-palettes-sixty-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-palettes-sixty-degree): Apply smooth palette transport to the equilateral decomposition of a unit direction. -> Almost-everywhere disjointness of consecutive rotated palettes.
- **step-binary-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-separation): Derive state-preserving circle diffeomorphisms from nearby binary centers, enlarge density intervals with bounded distortion, and propagate a conull interval around the circle. -> An absolute positive separation between distinct binary centers for one pair.
- **step-palette-cardinality** [claim](#stmt-prop-palette-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-palette-size): A triple palette would force a positive-measure set of complementary-pair binary centers on a compact circle, contradicting their uniform separation. -> Nonempty palettes of at most two labels at every fixed center almost everywhere.
- **step-transition-avoiding-traces** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-avoiding-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-avoiding-trace): Use circle-measure Fourier decay and dominated convergence to control translated circle averages, then select transition roots with vanishing endpoint-label circle mass. -> An avoiding trace for each transition edge.
- **step-strict-state-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-strict-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-strict-separation): Create singleton density points from complementary represented groups and use a perturbed angular correspondence to contradict a limiting unit chord in the other support. -> Strict support separation, uniform under the stated convergence.
- **step-two-state-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-two-state) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-two-state): Use an irrational rotation to find equal states, then form a unit equilateral triangle whose vertices must use the complementary label pair. -> Impossibility of the singleton–pair alternating state system.
- **step-four-cycle-state-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-four-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-four-continuity): Bound canonical state indicators by avoiding-trace coordinates and identify all weak-star limits using the palette size bound. -> Strong angular convergence of canonical state indicators along the locus.
- **step-global-angular-step-ban** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-step-ban) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-step-ban): Build separate avoiding traces at a translated support-contact center and apply transport with a constant second endpoint. -> Both corresponding angular transitions are absent almost everywhere.
- **step-cyclic-center-local-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-thm-cyclic-local-finiteness) / [proof](#pm-h-35): Combine closed edge loci with separate nonaccumulation proofs: binary separation for triangles, support transport for four-cycles, and global step bans for five-cycles. -> A closed locally finite cyclic-center locus.
- **step-disk-average-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-disk-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-disk-averages): Translate both endpoints of an exact unit pair by the same disk-distributed vector and average their typical-point exclusion. -> Pointwise unit-pair probability inequality and support detection.
- **step-missing-edge-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-transitions-missing-edge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transitions-missing-edge): Expand the product of disk averages over independent shifts and use the absent-edge transition modulus, then cover threshold violations by small grid disks. -> Small integrated overlap and disk covers with vanishing total radii.
- **step-small-hole-graph-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-interfaces-hole-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-hole-maps): Cover nonedge violations away from cyclic centers, merge hole disks, choose a diagonal smoothing sequence, and fill non-core boundaries through finite trees. -> Continuous graph-valued maps on the square minus core disks.
- **step-square-cover-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-interfaces-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-cover): Use a subordinate partition of unity to construct a near-identity map into finitely many segments, then force an open central region into its image by the planar fixed-point theorem. -> Contradiction to the proposed cover.
- **step-force-core-loop** [claim](#stmt-lem-interfaces-core-loop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-core-loop): Assume every core loop fills, form positive-coordinate component covers, and bound their mesh using exact unit crossings outside the small hole annuli. -> A non-null core boundary loop for every sufficiently large index.
- **step-reduced-circuit-midpoints** [claim](#stmt-lem-interfaces-graph-word) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-graph-word): Reduce edge paths using the covering tree, cyclically trim conjugations, and compare loops avoiding an edge midpoint with nonzero circuit powers. -> A simple cycle and midpoint detection for its circuit edges.
- **step-annular-midpoint-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lem-interfaces-annular-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-annular-crossing): If no midpoint-preimage component crosses the annulus, separate its contacts by a compact partition and extract a grid-boundary loop of nonzero winding avoiding the midpoint. -> A compact connected midpoint preimage meeting both boundaries.
- **step-common-exclusion-continua** [claim](#stmt-prop-interface-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interfaces-annular-crossing-2): Select a repeated core center and simple label cycle, take Hausdorff limits of midpoint crossings, and combine exact unit crossings with disk-average bounds to exclude both endpoint supports. -> Common-center compact connected exclusion sets and limiting directions.
- **step-one-sided-strips** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-strips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-strips): Use selected continuum points approaching the common center to produce strict inner and outer distance straddles, then take separate radial traces from both sides. -> Nonempty allowed sets, open restricted strips, and palette intersections.
- **step-exclude-five-cycle** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-five) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-five): Classify positive and negative edge-sign runs and compare antipodal palette options with successive disjoint diagonal steps on the rotation orbit. -> The extracted cycle cannot have length five.
- **step-four-cycle-word-alternatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-words): Analyze outsider positions using adjacent inequality and antipodal diagonal equality; rule out isolated mixed-parity words through dense angular chains. -> One outsider parity in each word and splitting of every generic direction.
- **step-four-ray-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-four-rays) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-four-rays): Compare selected ray multiplicities across perpendicular boundaries and identify the complementary projective arcs for the forced diagonals. -> Two distinct antipodal ray pairs and a short projective diagonal arc.
- **step-exclude-four-cycle** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-four): Show that changing outsider parity requires incompatible projective arc lengths; dense-chain propagation then contradicts the parity reversal under rotation. -> The extracted cycle cannot have length four.
- **step-triangle-local-binary-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-binary-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-binary-local): Build even unit-edge paths through two outsider-only strips, infer local essential color constancy by Fubini, and pass to an angular trace. -> Local essential constancy of the outsider angular state away from the specified perpendicular directions.
- **step-triangle-boundary-orbit** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-boundary): Use rotation invariance of the essential boundary and count its required perpendicular projective classes. -> Exactly one boundary orbit and alternating outsider sectors.
- **step-polynomial-region** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-region) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-region): Rewrite the polynomial inequality as negativity of a product of triple-angle sines, then transport opposite outsider exclusions through the nonsingular unit-direction sum map. -> An open neighborhood restricted almost everywhere to the triangle labels.
- **step-seven-point-certificate** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-certificate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-three-3): Enclose radicals by rational endpoints, propagate coordinate errors through the rational placement, and compare rational polynomial upper and lower bounds for every vertex. -> Strict radius and polynomial membership for all placed vertices.
- **step-exclude-triangle** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-three) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-three): Choose a common small translation avoiding the finitely many exceptional sets so the placed Moser graph becomes properly colored by the triangle labels. -> The extracted cycle cannot have length three.
- **step-no-weak-five-coloring** [claim](#stmt-thm-weak-five) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-three-2): Combine interface-cycle existence with the exclusion of every permitted cycle length. -> No weak measurable five-coloring exists.
- **step-main-plane-chromatic-bounds** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-weak-five): Transfer a hypothetical proper five-coloring to the forbidden weak coloring and verify the triangular-lattice seven-coset hexagonal construction for the upper bound. -> The unrestricted plane chromatic number lies between six and seven.
- **step-positive-badness** [claim](#stmt-cor-positive-badness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-positive-badness): Use graph compactness to obtain a finite obstruction, average its unavoidable monochromatic edge, and apply the stated measurable frequency consequences. -> An existential common positive badness lower bound.

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-multiple-averages",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $X$ is an ergodic standard probability-preserving $K$-system, that $Y$ is an invariant factor, and that $X\\times_YX$ is ergodic. Let $P=\\mathbb E(\\,\\cdot\\mid Y)$. For pairwise distinct integers $n_1,\\ldots,n_l$ and bounded functions $v_1,\\ldots,v_l$ on $X$, $$\\label{eq:source-6}\n \\operatorname{Avg}_{u\\in\\Phi_N}\n \\left\\|P\\left(\\prod_{j=1}^lU_{u^{n_j}}v_j\\right)\n       -\\prod_{j=1}^lU_{u^{n_j}}Pv_j\\right\\|_2^2\n \\longrightarrow0$$ along every Følner sequence $(\\Phi_N)$ in $K$. The pair criterion used in the proof is, for bounded $v,w$ and $d\\ne0$, $$\\label{eq:source-7}\n \\operatorname{Avg}_{u\\in\\Phi_N}\n \\lVert P(vU_{u^d}w)-Pv\\,U_{u^d}Pw\\rVert_2^2\\longrightarrow0.$$",

        }

The operators on this subspace extend to a strongly continuous unitary representation $T_x$, $x\\in\\mathbb R^2$, which preserves the positive cone and the constant one.*\n\n*If the system also has rotations $V_u$, $u\\in K$, satisfying $V_uT_zV_u^{-1}=T_{uz}$, then $\\mathcal H_c$ and its orthogonal projection are rotation invariant.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-colorability-transfer**: Existence of a proper coloring and existence of a weak measurable coloring are equivalent for every positive integer number of colors in ZFC.
- **inv-palette-cardinality**: At every fixed center, almost every angular palette is nonempty and contains at most two labels.
- **inv-seven-point-membership**: Every placed Moser vertex satisfies the strict squared-radius conditions and the polynomial inequality defining the three-label region.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-network-lower-bound**: Require six labels for every finite network embedded in the plane. -> The plane-wide obstruction does not say that every finite subgraph has that chromatic requirement.
- **anti-pointwise-weak-properness**: Treat an arbitrary representative of a weak measurable coloring as pointwise proper. -> Exceptional unit pairs are permitted, including those affected by changes on a plane-null set.
- **anti-common-angular-exception-set**: Use a single conull angular set simultaneously at every center. -> The palette cardinality assertion permits a different exceptional angular set at each center.
- **anti-finite-certificate-global-proof**: Treat successful verification of the placed vertices as verification of the entire plane-coloring theorem. -> The finite certificate checks a geometric input to the triangle contradiction; it does not check spectral rigidity, the transfer, or interface extraction.
- **anti-boundary-membership**: Include the boundary of the polynomial region in the certificate. -> The neighborhood conclusion uses strict polynomial and squared-radius inequalities.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The seven-point membership certificate is finite and explicitly constructive. Its rational coordinate boxes and polynomial bounds permit exact verification of the specified placement. This checks the geometric assertion of the seven-point certificate, not the entire non-colorability proof.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object with vertex equal to one of 0, A, B, T, uA, uB, uT.
# PRECONDITIONS: The fixed algebraic vertices and fixed placement from the seven-point certificate.
# OUTPUTS: A JSON object identifying the vertex and reporting certified strict region membership.
# TIME: A bounded collection of exact integer and rational operations per specified vertex.
# SPACE: A bounded collection of integer and rational values per specified vertex.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic and exact integer checks of radical enclosures.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; a failed certificate produces certified false.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction as Q
from flint import fmpz

h = Q('0.00011')
ROWS = {
 '0': ('-1.2906','0.1876','0.90','1.35','0.0353','4.963','1.70','1.71'),
 'A': ('-0.6335','0.9414','0.095','0.110','0.887','0.319','1.28','1.29'),
 'B': ('-0.3092','-0.0045','0.000002','0.00065','0.000022','0.287','0.095','0.096'),
 'T': ('0.3478','0.7493','0.023','0.026','0.562','0.199','0.682','0.683'),
 'uA': ('-1.1598','1.1790','9.8','19.0','1.391','2.647','2.73','2.74'),
 'uB': ('-0.3666','0.5700','0.0022','0.024','0.326','0.079','0.459','0.46'),
 'uT': ('-0.2358','1.5614','12.65','12.9','2.44','2.273','2.493','2.495')
}

def add(a,b):
 return (a[0]+b[0],a[1]+b[1])

def scale(a,c):
 c=Q(c)
 return (a[0]*c,a[1]*c) if c>=0 else (a[1]*c,a[0]*c)

def point(v):
 v=Q(v)
 return (v,v)

def radical_box(n,lo,hi):
 lo,hi=Q(lo),Q(hi)
 assert fmpz(lo.numerator)**2 < fmpz(n)*fmpz(lo.denominator)**2
 assert fmpz(hi.numerator)**2 > fmpz(n)*fmpz(hi.denominator)**2
 return (lo,hi)

def placed_boxes(label):
 a=radical_box(3,'1.73205','1.73206')
 b=radical_box(11,'3.31662','3.31663')
 ab=(a[0]*b[0],a[1]*b[1])
 vertices={
  '0':(point(0),point(0)),
  'A':(scale(a,Q(1,2)),point(Q(1,2))),
  'B':(scale(a,Q(1,2)),point(Q(-1,2))),
  'T':(a,point(0)),
  'uA':(scale(add(scale(a,5),scale(b,-1)),Q(1,12)),scale(add(ab,point(5)),Q(1,12))),
  'uB':(scale(add(scale(a,5),b),Q(1,12)),scale(add(ab,point(-5)),Q(1,12))),
  'uT':(scale(a,Q(5,6)),scale(ab,Q(1,6)))
 }
 gx,gy=vertices[label]
 gx=add(gx,point(Q(-290,250)))
 gy=add(gy,point(Q(149,250)))
 x=scale(add(scale(gx,35),scale(gy,-12)),Q(1,37))
 y=scale(add(scale(gx,12),scale(gy,35)),Q(1,37))
 return x,y

def certify(label):
 cx,cy,pplus,pminus,by,bH,lower,upper=map(Q,ROWS[label])
 x,y=placed_boxes(label)
 enclosed=(cx-h < x[0] <= x[1] < cx+h and cy-h < y[0] <= y[1] < cy+h)
 if not enclosed or not (abs(cx)>h and abs(cy)>h):
  return False
 xl,xu=(abs(cx)-h)**2,(abs(cx)+h)**2
 yl,yu=(abs(cy)-h)**2,(abs(cy)+h)**2
 ll,lu=xl+yl,xu+yu
 H=max(abs(3*xl-yu),abs(3*xu-yl))
 factors=(yu<by and H<bH and lower<ll<=lu<upper)
 radius=(0 < lower < upper and upper < 4 and (upper<1 or lower>1))
 pup=by*bH**2
 plow=lower**3*(1-upper/4)*min(abs(lower-1),abs(upper-1))**2
 separation=(pup<pplus and pplus < pminus and pminus<plow)
 return bool(enclosed and factors and radius and separation)

request=json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(request,dict) or set(request)!={'vertex'} or request['vertex'] not in ROWS:
 raise ValueError('invalid certificate vertex')
label=request['vertex']
print(json.dumps({'vertex':label,'certified':certify(label)},sort_keys=True))

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The displayed rational coordinate boxes and polynomial bounds support a finite regression check for the placed geometric obstruction. This checks the certificate rather than the full plane-coloring theorem. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The unit-distance obstruction constrains color assignments on the Euclidean plane. It supplies no game dynamics, collision-response rule, or simulation update procedure. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The rational tables provide a concrete input contract for a certificate checker that accepts only when coordinate enclosure and strict polynomial separation both hold. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The certificate gives a finite arithmetic procedure for verifying the explicit placement. The residue argument also specifies how signed inputs can be accumulated under a cap, but the placement checker has a fully displayed finite input. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite graph argument establishes reduced-word and midpoint-detection properties, but it does not specify a storage representation or data-structure operations with computational guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The concrete certificate reduces to rational arithmetic, while the surrounding proof uses probability factors and limiting arguments. Neither supplies an accelerator architecture or a hardware execution contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-exact-euclidean-distance** (formal_scope): The coloring contract concerns exact Euclidean unit distance on the entire plane. Other metrics, discrete subsets, and proximity constraints require separate results. Blocked use: ['Applying the plane lower bound to every finite spatial network.', 'Treating exact unit-distance exclusion as a collision rule for all nearby points.']
- **limit-chromatic-number-unresolved** (unknown): The paper leaves six and seven as unresolved alternatives for the chromatic number. Blocked use: ['Reporting an exact chromatic number from the stated bounds.']
- **limit-weak-coloring-exceptional-pairs** (formal_scope): Weak measurable properness excludes monochromatic unit pairs in product measure; it does not exclude every individual unit pair. Pointwise unit independence is established for density-one points. Blocked use: ['Using arbitrary assigned values at exceptional points as a proper coloring.', 'Replacing product-measure exclusion with an everywhere assertion.']
- **limit-transfer-existence-and-compactness** (nonconstructive): The transfer is an existence equivalence in ZFC. Its converse uses compactness after coloring translated finite configurations and does not specify a computable pointwise repair of the supplied weak coloring. Blocked use: ['Implementing a conversion of an arbitrary weak coloring into a proper coloring solely from the equivalence.']
- **limit-badness-constant-existential** (nonconstructive): The finite obstruction and universal positive badness constant are existential. A numerical value for the constant is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Publishing a numerical universal badness threshold.', 'Claiming that the displayed Moser graph obstructs five colors.']
- **limit-badness-frequency-regimes** (hypothesis): The periodic frequency conclusion requires measurable color classes and independent periods. The square-table conclusion assumes convergence of the specified conditional frequencies. Blocked use: ['Claiming convergence for every measurable coloring.', 'Applying the periodic sampling conclusion without a fundamental parallelogram.']
- **limit-wild-rigidity-input** (hypothesis): Haar rigidity requires an invariant probability on the specified character group that gives zero mass to ordinary continuous characters. Blocked use: ['Inferring Haar probability from rotation symmetry alone.', 'Applying the classification to a law with positive continuous-character mass.']
- **limit-invariant-line-average** (nonconstructive): The line-average argument uses an invariant mean on all bounded functions of the discrete quotient. Its vanishing assertion does not supply a finite numerical averaging procedure or assume ordinary measurability of the restricted Fourier coefficient. Blocked use: ['Replacing the invariant integral by sampled real-variable quadrature without a separate justification.']
- **limit-relative-singularity-tower** (hypothesis): Relative singularity requires an ergodic standard probability-preserving system, an equivariant measurable map with wild law, and a countable factor tower with the specified successor approximation property. Blocked use: ['Assuming conditional differences remain wild over an arbitrary factor.', 'Dropping the tower approximation property.']
- **limit-compact-tower-termination** (nonconstructive): The compact tower terminates at a countable ordinal by a separability argument. The proof does not give a finite stage bound or an effective enumeration of the factors. Blocked use: ['Treating countable termination as a finite runtime guarantee.']
- **limit-multiple-average-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Conditional decoupling requires ergodicity of the relative self-product, bounded functions, distinct integer powers, and a Følner sequence in the specified rotation group. No quantitative convergence rate is stated. Blocked use: ['Applying the conclusion to repeated powers or an arbitrary sampling sequence.', 'Inferring a finite stopping tolerance from asymptotic convergence.']
- **limit-capped-residue-regime** (hypothesis): The residue bound requires the difference inequality, a positive maximum input, finite input weights, bounded total integer coefficient length, and a final value below the cap. Blocked use: ['Using the bound for unrestricted sums outside the residue interval.', 'Treating infinite input weights as finite certificate data.']
- **limit-continuous-factor-positivity** (hypothesis): The positivity argument depends on a measure-preserving action with Koopman operators and identification of the continuous-vector subspace as a factor. An arbitrary spectral projection is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Assuming an arbitrary orthogonal projection preserves nonnegative label coordinates.', 'Assuming the extended operators supply a pointwise plane action.']
- **limit-fixed-center-angular-exceptions** (formal_scope): Angular palette assertions are made at fixed centers. Exceptional angular sets may depend on the center, trace, and transport maps; a common exceptional set over all centers is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Taking an uncountable union of angular exceptional sets and treating it as null.', 'Assuming joint measurability of palettes in their centers.']
- **limit-nonsingular-palette-transport** (hypothesis): Smooth transport requires nonparallel endpoint directions and a local angular diffeomorphism for the first endpoint map. The second endpoint map may be constant. Blocked use: ['Transporting angular null sets through a singular first endpoint map.', 'Extending the density conclusion to parallel endpoint directions.']
- **limit-five-label-geometric-stage** (formal_scope): The palette cardinality, cyclic-center analysis, and interface-cycle contradiction operate under the hypothetical weak measurable five-coloring. The transfer theorem's general color count does not extend these geometric conclusions automatically. Blocked use: ['Applying the same palette-size bound and cycle classification to an arbitrary number of labels.']
- **limit-missing-edge-overlap-asymptotic** (approximation): The missing-edge estimate is local and asymptotic on nested balls. Its threshold is fixed, and the resulting disk-cover radii vanish without a stated quantitative rate. Blocked use: ['Selecting a universal smoothing radius from the asymptotic overlap statement.', 'Applying the local estimate without the required containment of the smaller balls.']
- **limit-threshold-and-tree-fillings** (hypothesis): Threshold renormalization uses the stated positive threshold range. Tree fillings apply to non-core holes whose boundary images lie in forests; cyclic-center cores remain removed. Blocked use: ['Renormalizing at a threshold that permits a zero denominator.', 'Filling every core boundary through a tree without proving null homotopy.']
- **limit-annular-crossing-contract** (hypothesis): The crossing lemma requires a continuous map from the specified closed annulus into a finite connected simple graph, a nonempty reduced inner-boundary word, and an edge midpoint occurring in that word. Blocked use: ['Inferring a crossing continuum from an arbitrary graph-valued map.', 'Selecting an edge midpoint absent from the reduced boundary circuit.']
- **limit-extracted-cycle-and-continuum-shape** (formal_scope): The extracted label cycle need not be a transition-graph cycle at the common center. The connected sets and their selected limiting directions do not supply smooth interfaces or straight ray segments. Blocked use: ["Identifying every extracted edge with an edge of the common center's transition graph.", 'Replacing a continuum by a smooth boundary or a straight ray without proof.']
- **limit-strict-region-membership** (edge_case): The three-label neighborhood conclusion requires strict squared-radius and polynomial inequalities. Equality at the region boundary is outside the stated contract. Blocked use: ['Accepting boundary equality as certified membership.', 'Checking rounded coordinate centers instead of enclosing the actual placed vertices.']
- **limit-finite-certificate-coverage** (formal_scope): The finite placement certificate checks the specified Moser vertices. It does not verify spectral rigidity, coloring transfer, continuum extraction, or the existence of three-label neighborhoods. Blocked use: ['Reporting a successful finite membership check as verification of the entire plane-coloring theorem.']
- **limit-common-translation-selection** (nonconstructive): The final coloring contradiction selects a common translation outside exceptional null sets. Certified coordinates alone do not identify such a translation for arbitrary measurable classes. Blocked use: ['Reading arbitrary color values at the fixed placed coordinates as the final proper three-coloring.', 'Treating the coordinate certificate as an algorithm for locating typical colored vertices.']

A *weak measurable $k$-coloring* is a Lebesgue measurable function $c:\\mathbb R^2\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, with color classes $A_i=c^{-1}(\\{i\\})$, such that, for every $R>0$, $$\\sum_{i=1}^k\\int_{B(0,R)}\\int_{S^1}\n  \\mathbf 1_{A_i}(x)\\mathbf 1_{A_i}(x+u)\\,d\\sigma(u)\\,dx=0.$$ The integrals use the completed product measure; equivalently, one may first choose Borel representatives of the color classes forming a partition of the plane.",
          "source_id": "PM-158.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-palettes-density-proper",
          "exact_quote": "In a weak measurable $k$-coloring with classes $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$, the full-density-point set of each $A_i$ contains no unit pair. These density-point sets are disjoint and their union is conull.",
          "source_id": "PM-158.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-weak-coloring-exceptional-pairs",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "Weak measurable properness excludes monochromatic unit pairs in product measure; it does not exclude every individual unit pair. Pointwise unit independence is established for density-one points.",

Scope note of family 158 (`lean/docs/158.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Euclidean plane is not five-colorable](../../preprints/The-Euclidean-plane-is-not-five-colorable-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Hadwiger–Nelson problem asks for the fewest colors needed to color the plane so that points at distance one have different colors. The formalized results prove that five colors do not suffice and that seven colors do suffice. The lower bound applies to arbitrary colorings, with no measurability or continuity assumption; the upper bound includes every boundary point of the coloring regions.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace EuclideanFiveColor

def ProperColoring (colorCount : ℕ) (coloring : ℂ → Fin colorCount) : Prop :=
  ∀ point otherPoint : ℂ, ‖point - otherPoint‖ = 1 → coloring point ≠ coloring otherPoint

theorem no_proper_five_coloring : ¬ ∃ coloring : ℂ → Fin 5, ProperColoring 5 coloring := by
  sorry

end EuclideanFiveColor

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every coloring of the Euclidean plane with five colors has a monochromatic unit-distance pair, with no regularity assumption on the color classes. Consequently, the chromatic number of the plane is either six or seven.
